## 1. Header — provenance, posture, the reading rule

**Provenance.** The fifteen parse-that module apotheoses — `pt-ansi · pt-core · pt-debug ·
pt-diagnostics · pt-index · pt-lazy · pt-leaf · pt-packrat · pt-packrat-entry · pt-parser ·
pt-parsers · pt-split · pt-state · pt-utils · pt-utils-entry` — are adjudicated and banked at
`docs/tranches/V/megatranche/registry/adjudicated/pt-*.md`, the set completed by commit
**`1a3e9af1`** ("PARSE-THAT ADJUDICATION COMPLETE 15/15 MODULES — the X·P refinement-fold input
set is whole"). This addendum is the M-23/M-27 ¶2 fold of that whole input set onto the X·P wave
corpus.

**E-3 posture (addenda, never patch).** The dated wave specs
`docs/tranches/X/parse-that/waves/W0.md..W4.md` and `waves/CONFORMANCE-2026-08-03.md` are **NEVER
edited**. This file is the fold's **sole write**. Where this addendum strikes or corrects a phrase
in a dated spec, the strike is recorded here and the spec's bytes stand untouched — the dated
record is immutable; the addendum is the later adjudicated word.

**The reading rule.** At execution, each wave reads **its dated spec PLUS this addendum**, in that
order, and where the two disagree the addendum's clause governs. Every W1 measurement seat
additionally reads §7 (MEASURE-AT-OPEN) as the single authoritative wave-open measurement list.
Nothing here re-stages, re-weights, or re-composites the specs' gates (OC-2/CC-096 staging stands);
this addendum sharpens predicates, adds corpus rows and riders, and records exclusions.

**Fold classes** (the collator's, kept): **cl.1** = spec sharpening / law / correction · **cl.2**
= cure-lock / binding contract clause · **cl.3** = MEASURE-AT-OPEN row (§7) · **cl.4** = EXCLUDED,
does not fold (§8).

with the wrong home) and the merge register, pass 3 the LIMB and POINTER axes (limbs of two-wave
rows, internal cross-references, and the epistemic status of every "measured" token). **Pass 4

whole**: the IDENTITY axis by building the table of every source row appearing in more than one
residence (n.17, and n.4 rewritten as the register OF that table), and the LIMB axis by extracting

THAN THE CORPUS'S OWN SPELLINGS** — n.18's fold-token list omits `REFOLD` (pt-index's spelling) and
`**RF**`/`NWO` (pt-packrat-entry's), and n.17's §8 expansion treated bloc 2's class-quantifier

   **THE REGISTER OF THE TABLE** (rewritten whole by the L-20 **pass-4** repair, n.17). Three
   passes wrote a completeness claim here and three checks falsified it — "the ONE pair that is
   MERGED" (pass 1), "THE COMPLETE MERGE REGISTER" (pass 2), "completed again" (pass 3) — each time
   because the register was assembled from the rows the pass happened to look at, never from the
   table. **THE WORD "COMPLETE" IS STRUCK, AND WITH IT THE HABIT.** What follows is not a claim
   about this document; it is a PARTITION of it. Every one of the **132** provenance lines was
   extracted and assigned exactly ONE class, by a stated mechanical rule, and the three classes sum
   to 132. Any checker re-running the extraction lands on this same partition or names the row it
   disagrees about.
   **THE RULE.** *MERGE* = every source row the line names is drawn from ONE apotheosis (a
   within-module collapse), **or** the line itself declares an identity ("MERGED", "same identity",
   "cross-book"). *CROSS-FOLD* = the line names source rows from TWO OR MORE apotheoses with no
   declared single identity: many witnesses of one law, each contributing its own evidence.
   *SINGLE-SOURCE* = the line names ONE source roster row; anything else it names (a seat ruling,
   a killed claim, a superlative, a MEASURE number, a Closing verdict, a receipt code) is EVIDENCE
   INSIDE THAT ONE BOOKING and is not a second source row. Orthogonally, a line may carry a
   **CITATION** — a source row named but NOT booked here, struck to a pointer at its real booking
   per the n.9 idiom; the citation-bearing lines are listed last and are counted in whichever of
   the three classes their remaining names put them.
   **THE RULE COMPLETED OVER A CASE IT NEVER COVERED (pass 6, 2026-08-08 — n.21), AND THE SCOPE OF
   ITS IDENTITY CLAUSE STATED.** Two silences in the rule as passes 4 and 5 wrote it made the
   partition unreproducible, and both are closed here in the open rather than by moving a row to
   fit: **(a) ZERO-ROSTER-ROW LINES.** Three provenance lines name NO source roster row at all —
   their whole content is what the rule itself calls evidence, drawn from ONE apotheosis. The rule
   assigned them no class, so they are given their own, **EVIDENCE-ONLY**, and enumerated below; a
   checker who rejects the fourth class and reads the rule strictly gets **41 · 16 · 72 with three
   lines unclassified** (41 + 16 + 72 + 3 = 132 either way), which is the same partition minus a
   name. **(b) THE IDENTITY CLAUSE IS
   ABOUT THE ROWS THE LINE BOOKS.** "The line itself declares an identity" means the line declares
   that the SOURCE ROWS IT NAMES are one row (so one holding is booked); it is not triggered by the
   word "identity" appearing about something else. Two lines turn on this and are stated so the
   reading can be checked: **W1.3** ("RC-3/RC-4 — strengthens the SAME row, one identity") declares
   an identity between two receipt codes and pt-parsers PT-PARS-12, not among its six modules' rows,
   so it stays a CROSS-FOLD; **W2.49** (⟨pt-packrat.md · sibling L-axis identity map⟩) names an
   artefact called an identity map and no roster row at all, so it is EVIDENCE-ONLY, not a MERGE.

18. **THE LIMB SWEEP, RUN WHOLE — the table first, then the repairs (L-20 pass-4 repair).**
    n.13's limb sweep read "every two-wave source disposition" and banked two gaps; the pass-4
    check proved it had missed at least five. **METHOD, STATED SO IT CAN BE CHECKED**: every roster
    row of all fifteen apotheoses was extracted with its TERMINAL DISPOSITION and classified by the
    target tokens that disposition names — `W0`·`W1`·`W2`·`W3`·`W4`·`NO-WAVE-OWNER`·
    `MEASURE-AT-OPEN`·`REFINEMENT FOLD`.

    VOCABULARY, AND THE SENTENCE BELOW CALLING THE READING "DELIBERATELY WIDE" IS FALSE FOR TWO OF
    THE FIFTEEN.** Two apotheoses spell the same targets differently and NONE of their spellings is
    in the list: **pt-index writes `REFOLD`** (17 occurrences) and **pt-packrat-entry writes
    `**RF**`** (12) **and `NWO`** (22, for NO-WAVE-OWNER). Machine-counted across all fifteen files,
    those three spellings occur in NO other apotheosis (0/0/0 in the other thirteen), so the
    narrowness is confined to these two — but on those two the sweep was narrow, not wide, and one
    fold-class row fell out of it entirely: **pt-index M-8 (+C-M3)**, whose disposition is

    {`REFINEMENT FOLD`, `REFOLD`, `**RF**`, `NWO`} is stated at n.21 with its residual.)*

    the index could not home on some limb was read by hand against its source. **The reading is

    **THE METHOD LESSON, STATED IN THE OPEN — AND WHY THIS ONE IS TERMINAL.** Three consecutive
    passes each widened the FORMAT LIST and each was convicted by the next format: pass 6 swept one
    spelling and was convicted by three; pass 7 swept table cells and was convicted by bullets;
    pass 8's predecessor swept tables-and-bullets and was convicted by prose and by an escape
    character. A format list is an enumeration of what the sweeper has already imagined, so each
    such pass buys exactly one round. **The census is complete only when the extraction is keyed on
    ROSTER-ROW IDS rather than on markup shapes** — enumerate the rows the roster declares, then
    read each row's disposition — because that is the one key the corpus itself is written in, and
    it is invariant under every future re-formatting of any apotheosis. **The id-keyed derivation is
    the terminal method, and a later pass that widens a format list rather than keying on ids
    should be read as having re-opened this gap, not closed it.**
    **THE REVERSAL, IN THE OPEN — AND THIS ROUND'S LESSON.** (A) relabelled §9's pass-5 sentence
    "**∅ FORWARD** (75 `REFINEMENT FOLD` rows, all booked)" as NARROW and its "all booked" as FALSE
    once. **That relabel is itself the narrow one, and is REVERSED here**: **75** is the CORRECT
    count of `REFINEMENT FOLD` rows over both roster formats, and pass 5's "all booked" is **TRUE**
    — pt-index `M-8 (+C-M3)`, the one drop, spells its fold `REFOLD` (`pt-index.md:63`), so it was
    never inside the 75 and could not falsify them. Pass 5's real gap was SCOPE (one spelling of
    three); pass 6 cured the spelling and re-opened the same gap on the other axis, landing **18
    rows BELOW pass 5** on the shared spelling. **The extraction, not the spelling, was the gap.**
    The receipt that convicts (A) from inside this file is n.18's own limb table, which had already
    read both bullet rosters (pt-debug 22 · pt-split 10, `:665-667`). The one drop and its cure at
    **W2.80** are untouched by the reversal: pt-index `M-8` was, and remains, the only unbooked
    fold-limb row any pass has found, and it is booked.
    **(B) BLOC 2's CLASS-QUANTIFIER, EXPANDED TO IDS.** §8 bloc 2 ended "`M-1, M-5 + the MINOR/INFO
    tail`" — the only register entry naming rows by SEVERITY CLASS. n.17's expansion is literal and
    n.20's sweep is over ranges, so neither could reach it, and the phrase occurred exactly once in
    this file and was treated nowhere. Expanded at the bytes, the tail is **24 rows**: fifteen carry
    an `NWO` limb and are kept, and **nine do not and are TRIMMED** — seven `**RF**`-only rows, each
    folded whole in a wave section (L§9 PT-07 → W1.2 · C-10 → W4.13 · M-2 → W2.50 · M-7 → W1.12 ·
    M-8 → W4.13 · M-9 → W4.13 · M-10 → W4.13), and two routed `W1 · MEASURE-AT-OPEN` and carried at
    §7 (M-3 → §7 pt-packrat-entry row 2 · M-6 → row 3). **The n.20 test governs the choice and it
    is applied, not assumed**: trim where a real booking exists elsewhere (all nine), DECLARE where
    a trim would drop the row (bloc 16's PT-UE-29/-31, pass 5). Three of the fifteen kept rows are
    ALSO folded and gain a DECLARED TWO-LIMB residence in n.17's table — **E-B1** (W1.12 · bloc 2),
    **E-M4** (W1.14) and **E-m4** (W1.15). **n.17's table is therefore 64, not 54** (17
    provenance-side · 47 boundary; 29 DECLARED TWO-LIMB · 10 CITATION-NOT-BOOKING · 8 §8
    OVER-REACH), with **56** live dual residences and eight rows that are records of a cure.
    **(C) THE PARTITION RE-DERIVED, AND THE RULE COMPLETED WHERE IT WAS SILENT.** n.4's pass-5

    FALSE: five lines disagree, all by the doctrine pass 5 itself stated at W4.7 — *the rule

    names ONE roster row, pt-leaf C-i2, the rest being rulings, killed claims and a dissent, which

    whose whole provenance is adjudicated evidence from one apotheosis — and the strict reading

    OVER ALL 132 LINES AT THE POST-REPAIR BYTES: 41 MERGE · 16 CROSS-FOLD · 72 SINGLE-SOURCE ·
    3 EVIDENCE-ONLY = 132**, no line unclassified and none in two classes. The pass-5 triple

    **(D) WHAT THIS PASS DOES NOT CLAIM.** It re-ran three sweeps and re-read every row it moved,

## 2. W0 — Pause-State Verification and the Fresh Root (2 sharpenings)

**W0.1 · pt-leaf PT-LEAF-14 — src-consumption load-order proof obligation** (cl.1)
`export let whitespace` (non-optional, never initialized in-module) + cross-module top-level
`_initWhitespace()` under `sideEffects:false` — correctness hangs on **barrel statement order**,
and an ESM entry evaluating `leaf.js` first assigns a still-TDZ `let` and throws at src level
(bundle-scope safe: single chunk). **CURE-LOCK on the fresh root**: any consumer of the fresh
root's **src** carries a **LOAD-ORDER PROOF OBLIGATION**; otherwise it consumes **dist only,
STATED**. The 3-symbol cure is killed: `parser.ts:6` imports FIVE and the two omitted
(`whitespace`, `_initWhitespace`) ARE the deferral. (The in-cycle-mutable-exports ban binds the
candidate at W2 — see W2.16.)
*Sharpens*: W0.c (W0.md:241-268) — the unit rightly builds/installs/executes **nothing**
("the toolchain is X.P.W1's", W0.md:262-264); this row makes the consumption posture an explicit
obligation on the **first consuming wave** (W1), not an accident of W0's abstinence.
⟨pt-leaf.md · PT-LEAF-14⟩

**W0.2 · pt-utils-entry PT-UE-9 (W0 limb) — a TIER PROMISE IS AN IMPORT-GRAPH CLAIM, and the fresh
root inherits a FALSE one** (cl.1 — **W0 limb recovered by the L-20 fold check, §1a n.10**)
The clone opens at a commit whose own tier promise is already false: `core.ts:4-5` denies loading
the diagnostics and packrat tiers, and **BOTH of its first two clauses are false for `/core`
itself** — the packrat chunk imports the diagnostics chunk, so **every subpath inherits both**, and
the defect is FAMILY-LEVEL (causes: `vite.config.ts`'s five entries with no `manualChunks`, plus
`src/parse/utils.ts` fusing the diagnostics accumulator at `:6-148` with the byte scanners at
`:150-186`). **FOLD ON W0**: nothing in the fresh root's census or routing declaration may restate
an in-tree tier promise AS FACT — a promise about what a tier does not load is an **IMPORT-GRAPH
CLAIM**, discharged only by walking the graph, **never by file existence + `typeof`** (the W4.1
vacuity class). W0 opens the root and asserts nothing about it; this row makes that abstinence
**stated posture rather than accident**, and hands the assertion instrument to the wave that first
consumes the tier. (The W4 limb — the release gate loading the shipped entry in a bare process and
asserting the LOADED-MODULE SET under both conditions, with the byte closures — is owned by
**W4.2**; identity: pt-leaf **PT-LEAF-17**'s W4 rider, per this row's own disposition.)
*Sharpens*: W0.c (W0.md:241-268, "nothing in the fresh root is built, installed, or executed in
this wave", W0.md:262-264) and W0.d's routing declaration (W0.md:270-291) — a declaration that
repeated `core.ts:4-5` would carry a falsehood forward under W0's own signature.
⟨pt-utils-entry.md · PT-UE-9 (W0 limb; the W4 limb at W4.2)⟩

## 3. W1 — Harness, Corpus, and the Honest Bench (23 sharpenings)

**W1.1 · MERGED CROSS FOLD — the JS-boundary entry invariant** (cl.1)
ONE fold row, many witnesses: entry is via `parseState` + `isError` ONLY; `.parse()` truthiness
**BANNED**; the invariant asserts on `state.isError`, NEVER on the return (an undefined-check
passes every witness); and **NEVER READ `.value` UNDER `isError`** (`.value` on error is garbage
by construction, not merely undefined). Sharpeners: `many(1)`-FAIL and `many(0)`-SUCCESS both
yield truthy `[]`; a failed `then` returns the first sub-parser's residue; `minus()` leaves the
EXCLUDED parser's value; `quotedString()` returns an array from a declared `Parser<string>`;
`jsonParser` returns a truthy wrongly-typed partial on 6/6 rejected inputs; the published d.ts
`parse(val: string): T` is unsound under strictNullChecks. RIDER: adding `offset === src.length`
is necessary AND **MEASURED INSUFFICIENT** (csvParser corrupts with `isError=false` and
`offset===len` — see W1.19). Typed home already frozen: value.js `src/foundation/result.ts` +
ParseResult's non-empty failure tuple (`types.ts:25-27`).
*Sharpens*: W1 scope 9 (W1.md:106-107) and G-10's GUARD rows (W1.md:576-577) — every harness leg,
oracle, and corpus assertion in the lane is bound to this entry discipline.
⟨pt-core rows 2/19 · pt-leaf PT-LEAF-16 · pt-parser PT-PARSER-1 · pt-parsers PT-PARS-8/-11/-17/MISS-1 · pt-state PT-STATE-1 · pt-utils-entry PT-UE-1/-3 · pt-index m-11⟩

**W1.2 · MERGED CROSS FOLD — non-string boundary is positive typeof admission, never
throw-inference** (cl.1)
O-15 PT-07's "5/5 non-string inputs throw" is **REFUTED**; the boundary is three-to-four-valued:
throw / silent-reject / **SILENT-ACCEPT-COERCED** (`eof().parseState([])` → ok; `regex(/a*/)`
`test()`-coerces a number to an empty-match success; `whitespace.parseState(42)` → `isError:false`
via `0 >= undefined` → ToString; `skipWhitespace({src:42})` → silent no-op empty document; boxed
String silently works). W2's boundary clause is a **POSITIVE `typeof x === "string"` admission
test at entry**, never a catch and never throw-inference; W1's boundary corpus gains the 8-probe
row and the 7-input × 5-callable matrix (message strings recorded, never asserted verbatim —
engine text). **Corrects W1.md:582's and W2.md:161-162's PT-07 readings** — the G-10 baseline
paste (W1.md:576, "5/5 throw raw TypeError") stands as a dated record; the *reading* of it is
amended here.
⟨pt-index B-3 · pt-leaf PT-LEAF-5 · pt-parser PT-PARSER-2 · pt-utils PT-UTILS-4 · pt-utils-entry PT-UE-25 · pt-packrat-entry L§9 PT-07⟩

**W1.3 · MERGED CROSS FOLD — the G-9 depth law** (cl.1)
G-9 asserts a **TYPED BOUND EXISTS**, never a constant. The normative sentence must bound declared
depth below the **COLD COMPOSED ceiling of the lane's deepest composed grammar** with stated
margin — never the bare-lazy 7,761 figure (a corpus declared at 5,000 satisfies G-9 literally and
crashes every JSON-shaped composed grammar). Depth baselines carry (shape, stack-size, JIT state,
prior-overflow) qualifiers. **STRUCK from the record this addendum governs: "the ceiling is a
stable property" — AT BOTH SITES THAT SHIP IT, `W1.md:553-554` AND `W2.md:832`** (G-11's pasted
born-RED baseline carries the identical clause; M11's disposition strikes it from **W1/W2** prose,
and the first fold pass named only the W1 site — **corrected by the L-20 pass-2 routing sweep,
§1a n.8**) — the corpus itself carries 7,787 (default) beside 7,761
(`--stack-size=984`), and a seat measured 1,580 COLD → 2,305 WARM in ONE process. RIDER (pt-debug
MISS-9): for any print path W2 keeps, **declare-your-WORK, not only declare-your-depth** —
`.then`/`.or` spines are 2^depth calls at frame depth = depth, so the observable failure at depth
≈30 is an unbounded HANG, never a RangeError.
**NEAR-DUPLICATE DECLARED (L-20 pass-4 repair, §1a n.18): pt-parser PT-PARSER-10 books HERE and
nowhere else.** Its disposition is `**W2** — depth bound as an algebra parameter; W1 G-9 owns the

row 18's `**W2** — K-7: the bound is an algebra parameter` (`pt-core.md:40`) — ONE holding, two

Its MEASURE limb rides §7 pt-parser row 5 (`Recursion ceiling — G-9 owns the row; 7,761/7,762
cited as a CLASS, never a constant`). No second booking is warranted and none is made.

Entry via `parseState` + `isError` ONLY — any harness leg reading `.parse()` truthiness

— the suite's ONLY idiom — passes on EVERY malformed vector. The tests certify a **FORK**:

**W1.5 · pt-parsers PT-PARS-2 — the W1 REJECTION-LEG LAW** (cl.1)
EVERY grammar in the lane's corpus carries a **REJECTION LEG**. An acceptance-only totality
harness scores a TOTAL-ACCEPT PARSER AT 100% — and this module is the existence proof: `csvParser`
has no rejection power structurally (`many(min=0)`'s only failure branch is unreachable; seat
census 0/500 + 0/8 curated), no record separator (README's own `a,b,c\n1,2,3` → ONE row), and
`",,,"` succeeds consuming ZERO of 3 bytes with an empty value and no error channel.

equivalence oracle at W1.md:233-256, whose imported MIRROR-DEFECT classes **B** `MIS_ACCEPT` and

**W1.6 · pt-parsers PT-PARS-15/-16/-19 — corpus riders** (cl.2)
(15) The lane's corpus carries **TRAILING-GARBAGE rows per grammar**, asserted via CONSUMED
OFFSET, never value shape — no `.eof()` anywhere in the module; every export is a prefix parser
presented as a document parser. (16) **CONTROL-CHAR rows**: raw U+0001–U+001F accepted inside JSON
strings, and `json.ts:23`'s `indexOf("\\")` fast path routes control-bearing backslash-free
strings AROUND the JSON.parse oracle BY CONSTRUCTION — a fast path must be probed **on the class
it skips the oracle for**. (19) Rejection-shape assertions in the lane bind `furthest`, NEVER

**W1.9 · pt-ansi row 2 / L-D2 (W1 witness-rider limb) — coloured-arm coverage rider** (cl.2)
`enabled` is a zero-way latch (import-time const, unexported, no setter, no FORCE_COLOR); the

addendum silently assumes: W1.3's depth law and §7's whole depth-ceiling family are unfalsifiable
without a depth-class assertion, and the ONE-CACHE spec (W2.30/W2.33 — "a harness cell must NEVER
`toString()` a grammar it also identity-measures") cannot be witnessed at all without a call count,
because running a thunk twice is invisible in every value the parse returns.

## 4. W2 — The Dual-Target Algebra (80 sharpenings)

### 4a. Algebra state, laws, and dispatch (pt-core + pt-ansi)

**W2.1 · pt-ansi row 1 / L-D1·C-D-4 — render policy INJECTED; the G-6 String(state) rider** (cl.1)
The render path takes `{color, sink}` as **INJECTED policy**; the ambient stderr oracle is retired
(all four §3c candidates pre-declare the kill — W2.md:304-408). The UNDECLARED tension folds as a
**NAMED RIDER on the equivalence scope**: is `String(state)` inside the compared drop-in surface?
`ParserState#toString()` is ANSI-decorated, so `String(state)` and everything over it is
TTY-dependent. *Sharpens*: EQ-4's rendering-non-normative note (W2.md:266) — the rider must be
answered where the compared surface is declared (W1.md:243-248's vendored-tarball rule).
⟨pt-ansi.md · adjudicated roster row 1⟩

**W2.2 · pt-core row 1 / B-1·C-2 — the AC-3 declared-divergence lock (nullable regex at EOF)**
(cl.2)
`regex()` short-circuits at EOF before the pattern is consulted, so every nullable pattern fails
at end of input — incl. the /core-exported `whitespace`. **AC-3's scan-union owns the `leaf.ts`
repair as a DECLARED DIVERGENCE ROW** for `.h` (repairing it changes `whitespace` for every
consumer; W2.md:444-447 Stage 3 + W2.md:524's scan-union seat). **W1 RIDER**: the equivalence
oracle's corpus gains inputs whose last token is a nullable regex — P-1's GREEN could not have
contained one.
⟨pt-core.md · roster row 1⟩

**W2.3 · pt-core row 4 / D-04·C-3 — the same AC-3 repair surface (no merge at EOF)** (cl.2)
The EOF arm of `regex()` never calls `mergeErrorState`, so `furthest` never advances and the
rendered error sits at offset 0. **Two /core leaves, two incompatible failure protocols. ONE cure,
ONE declared-divergence row shared with W2.2.** Commonest authoring error in the target domain
(truncated `oklch(0.5 0.1`).
⟨pt-core.md · roster row 4⟩

**W2.4 · pt-core row 6 / M2 — trim name-sniff, the EQ-1 divergence source** (cl.2)
`.trim(p)` SILENTLY DISCARDS `p` whenever `p.context?.name === "whitespace"`; the substituted
charCode loop is ASCII-only while `whitespace` = `/\s*/` includes NBSP/U+2000-200A. Keyed on a
mutable public string that is a sanctioned union member. **BINDING: the algebra wrap must not key
behavior on a mutable name; whitespace policy becomes an explicit operator parameter.**
*Sharpens*: the capability families (W2.md:246-251) and the closed enumeration (W2.md:139-142).
⟨pt-core.md · roster row 6⟩

**W2.5 · pt-core rows 8+9 / C-5 + D-11 — the R-LAW-3 discharge pair** (cl.2)
On /core, `expected` is ALWAYS undefined — `utils.ts:33/:38` gate the SEEDING itself — and /core
exports no enable/disable. The only cure (arming from /diagnostics) buys `parser.ts:67-69`'s
unconditional `console.error` on every failed parse; the gate lives inside `mergeErrorState`, not
at the render site. **The two rows DISCHARGE TOGETHER** (R-LAW-3, W2.md:281-283). An unwrapped
Parser cannot pass R-LAW-3's own console-patching probe.
⟨pt-core.md · roster rows 8 and 9⟩

**W2.6 · pt-core row 10 / D-12 — the O-8 anti-latch, sharpened at construction** (cl.2)
The packrat latch arms at wrapper **CONSTRUCTION** (`packrat.ts:290`), so it is process-global and
grammar-agnostic — one import-time `memoize()` anywhere taxes every unrelated parse forever;
`resetPackrat` never disarms (early-returns on the latch it never clears). **Arming becomes a
parse parameter** (O-8, W2.md:253-257). The 1.47× is O-15 N=1, never re-derived (§7).
⟨pt-core.md · roster row 10⟩

**W2.7 · pt-core rows 17 + 41/42 (SF-1) — dispatch fail-loud guard: one cure, three silent-drop
modes** (cl.2)
KEEP + WIDEN, **fail-loud at construction BEFORE any CSS head-table lands**: (a) non-ASCII table
keys silently dropped past 127 while the label still advertises them; (b) the 3-char range
heuristic fires on ANY `x-y` key, inverted ranges map nothing; (c) SF-1 (K17 REVERSED, live
witness): `internParser` mints one index per distinct parser ENTRY, so a 257-entry table
**SILENTLY MIS-DISPATCHES** (`isError` false, value from the wrong parser) and a 129-entry table
self-rejects. One guard discharges all three: `internParser` throws RangeError past 127 — the
`getCijKey` posture. *Sharpens*: the channel-table-dispatch family (W2.md:248).
⟨pt-core.md · roster rows 17, 41, 42 + Seat correction 1⟩

### 4b. pt-debug — the print/diagnostic tier at W2

**W2.8 · pt-debug B-1 = C-5 (MERGED with pt-ansi row 13 / M-1, §1a n.4/n.11) — the caret
coordinate contract** (cl.2, BLOCKER)
The caret pad is computed in ORIGINAL-source columns while `summarizeLine` re-origins the line;
displacement = `columnNum − start − 3`, unbounded; bites whenever the line exceeds 74 chars AND
the failure column > 37 (`start===0` aligns correctly — pt-ansi row 13's scope correction).
**The renderer WRAP's coordinate contract must gate the caret in RENDERED coordinates, killing the
class by construction.** Minified CSS is the library's stated domain and the X·P corpus shape; no
test pins a caret position. (Architecture/cure split with W3: see W2.17's C-11 clause.)
**MERGE + DUAL RESIDENCE, DECLARED (L-20 pass-3 repair)**: one defect read from two modules —
pt-debug B-1 routes it **`X.P.W2`** (the wrap contract kills the class by construction), pt-ansi
row 13 routes it **`NO-WAVE-OWNER` — render tier** (any relocation wave cures the incumbent by
shifting the caret by `start`). Both stand: **the candidate-side LAW folds HERE**, and **the
incumbent-side render-tier repair stays banked in §8 bloc 4**, where pt-ansi row 13 also lives —
declared dual residence, the same law-folds/cure-does-not split pt-core row 11 carries (§1a n.4).
⟨pt-debug.md B-1 (= pt-ansi.md row 13 / M-1, same identity — MERGED, §1a n.11; row 13's
render-tier cure banked at §8 bloc 4)⟩

**W2.9 · pt-debug M-10 — printer totality over the declared union** (cl.1)
The printer is information-free on 11 of the 29 declared context names (chain, dispatch, eof,
lookAhead, mapState, memoize, mergeMemo, minus, peek, recover, whitespace), each erasing its whole
subtree; all 11 are public surface; dispatch's args ARE the parsers, directly consumable by the
existing any/or arm. **FOLD CLAUSE: totality over the context-name union — a printer arm per
declared name, or a closed IR that cannot omit one.**
⟨pt-debug.md · M-10⟩

**W2.10 · pt-debug MISS-7 + M-3 — printer escaping and exclusion rendering** (cl.1)
The grammar printer emits its own metacharacters unescaped (`string('"')` prints `"""`; terminals
containing `| [ ] { } ! ? << >>` render as printer syntax) — output is ambiguous and not
round-trippable; CSS delimiter parsers are routine in the band's grammars. **Successor printer
spec: escaping or a quoted-terminal IR.** Companion: `a.not(b)` prints `!"a"` — the excluded
parser is dropped and the rendering asserts the negation of what the parser accepts; `a.not()`
prints identically. **The successor must render exclusion explicitly.**
⟨pt-debug.md · MISS-7 and M-3⟩

**W2.11 · pt-debug MISS-4 + MISS-5 — CRLF / trailing-whitespace fixture classes** (cl.1)
`summarizeLine`'s `trimEnd()` desynchronizes the caret on SHORT lines too — the lines both
challenges certify as correct: rendered content is trimmed, the pad is not, so every CRLF source
mis-points by ≥1 at end-of-line, overshoot unbounded. And the secondary block emits `lines[i]`
RAW, so on CRLF a bare `\r` returns the terminal cursor to column 0 and the marker line
OVERWRITES the source line. **The WRAP's coordinate contract must be GATED ON NAMED FIXTURE
CLASSES: CRLF + trailing-whitespace.**
⟨pt-debug.md · MISS-4 and MISS-5⟩

**W2.12 · pt-debug MISS-3 + i-3 — the Diagnostic value spec: source identity and field
decisions** (cl.1)
The `(Diagnostic, src)` API cannot enforce that `src` is the string the offsets came from — and
the package's own README worked example gets it wrong (collects from `cssWithErrors`, renders
against `css`); Diagnostic carries no source identity though `collectDiagnostic` had `state.src`
in hand, and the clamped renderer degrades to a confidently-wrong caret, never a throw. **The
Diagnostic-as-value spec CARRIES ITS SOURCE IDENTITY.** Same breath: `Diagnostic.offset` is
carried and never rendered — render it or drop the field (write-only; a wave mapping offset→start
would invent semantics). *Sharpens*: the D limb (W2.md:239); cross-ref W3.6 (nothing maps FROM
Diagnostic). **pt-diagnostics row 26 IS CROSS-REFERENCED HERE, NOT BOOKED (L-20 pass-4 repair,
§1a n.17)**: the source row declares itself "Rider on row 25's fold" (`pt-diagnostics.md:48`) and
row 25's fold is **W3.6**, which books both; this row states the write-only field's consequence
for the Diagnostic-as-value spec and cites it no more in provenance.
⟨pt-debug.md · MISS-3 and i-3 (pt-diagnostics row 26 → booked at W3.6, §1a n.17)⟩

**W2.13 · pt-debug MISS-10 — a memoized printer carries a context fingerprint** (cl.1)
`PARSER_STRINGS` caches on `parser.id` while `Parser.context` is public-mutable and the library
itself rewrites it post-construction (`eof()` reassigns `p.context`; `whitespace.context.name`
set post-init) — a cached rendering can never be invalidated. **Any memoized successor printer
carries a CONTEXT FINGERPRINT, not just a cycle guard.**
⟨pt-debug.md · MISS-10⟩

**W2.14 · pt-debug i-1 — observers go through the invocation path** (cl.1)
`parserDebug` bypasses `call()` — attaching `.debug()` could change what its subject accepts;
saved today only by a second dead mechanism (the flag machinery: sole `.flags=` write onto a
constructed-then-discarded Parser; `FLAG_EOF` has a declaration and a read and NO write site).
Whoever revives flags inherits the Heisenbug. **SPEC CLAUSE: observers go through the SAME
invocation path as execution.**
⟨pt-debug.md · i-1⟩

**W2.15 · pt-debug i-2 — falsy-id hygiene** (cl.1)
The printer's only cycle terminators boolean-read a numeric id (`if (!id)` / `if (id)`) against a
counter that starts at 0; latent only because `_initWhitespace()` at `parser.ts:711` permanently
owns id 0 — an accident of module-eval order a bundler may drop. **Cure: `id != null` /
`id === undefined` sentinel, never truthiness.** The same id-as-flag conflation is B-3's live
sibling.
⟨pt-debug.md i-2 · pt-lazy.md M5 · pt-state.md PT-STATE-28 · pt-index.md AD-10⟩

**W2.20 · pt-index B-4 + seat riders — whitespace RETIRE reason-weight (the trim escape-hatch

CURE-LOCK: **any successor whitespace terminal carries a BORN-RED EOF-acceptance gate.** Riders

`all(whitespace, this, whitespace)` at `parser.ts:484-485` BEFORE the name guard — a `.trim()`
call that IS broken — and that branch is cast `as unknown as Parser<T[]>`, a second tuple lie;

whitespace-shaped name in the closed 29-union silently swaps in the ASCII scanner (charCodes
9-13/32 — not even `/\s*/`'s Unicode class).

**W2.21 · pt-index AD-1 + AD-4 + m-3 — KEEP-riders that must be banned or guarded in the
candidate** (cl.2)
(AD-1) `regex(r, matchFunction)` can advance the offset **NEGATIVELY** — a failed sticky exec
resets `lastIndex` to 0 and a matchFunction returning non-empty on null (its DECLARED contract)
yields `ok(match, 0 − savedOffset)`, rewinding to 0; `many`/`sepBy`'s `===` zero-advance guards
are BLIND to it → unbounded loop from a published leaf; S-2 rescoped. Rider: `sticky.lastIndex`
is read AFTER the user callout returns — a re-entrant matchFunction silently corrupts the advance.
(AD-4) `mapState` hands user code a PROTOTYPE-CHAINED LIVE ALIAS of the NEW state as `oldState`,
typed `ParserState<T>`, minting a second hidden class; S-4 scoped to the default path. (m-3)
dispatch's key DSL: unbounded intern, idx 128-255 wraps negative (silent rejection), idx ≥256
wraps POSITIVE (routes to the WRONG parser), non-ASCII silently discarded, a literal `-` in a

`all()`'s tuple type lies — arity-1 unwrap plus drop-undefined at six sites make runtime arity

### 4e. pt-lazy at W2

**W2.30 · pt-lazy LZ-2 / M1 / M2 — the G-10/G-11 lazy-edge walk rule + the A.7 cure trap** (cl.1)
OPERATIONAL RULE RATIFIED: the harness descends a lazy edge via `args[0]` EXACTLY ONCE, caches at
the node, and NEVER via `getLazyParser` (which returns the phantom shadow graph); **ACROSS A LAZY
EDGE ASSERT PRESENCE, NEVER IDENTITY, ALLOCATION, OR TOTAL COUNT.** CURE TRAP (M1, found
independently by both readers, re-derived by the seat): challenge-C A.7's priced cure — populate
`context.parser` on a lazy node — **CRASHES `parserPrint` on every recursive grammar**
(`parserString` is resolved from `context.parser` BEFORE the switch; memo writes happen on the way
UP; the `case "lazy"` id-latch is the only cycle terminator and is reachable only BECAUSE
`context.parser` is undefined). The sound cure is a NEW accessor slot with the memo write moved
ABOVE the recursion; A.7's semver row re-prices from minor to REGRESSION. M2:
`expect(byName.get("lazy")).toBe(1)` is a LOWER BOUND — the census must pair graph-walk with
call-site grep and print both. *Sharpens*: G-10/G-11 (W2.md:808-838).
⟨pt-lazy.md · LZ-2, M1, M2 + seat ruling 4⟩

**W2.31 · pt-lazy LZ-8 / M3 — O-8/K-6 scope ALL user-callback seams; K-6 gains a steady-state
clause** (cl.1)
(a) The anti-latch construction rule must scope ALL user-callback seams, not lazy alone — `chain`
(`parser.ts:137`) invokes the continuation DURING the parse and may construct a `memoize()`;
`map`/`mapState` callbacks run inside the same `packratEnter` bracket. Exclusivity clauses in
L-M3 and N-1 are STRUCK. **W1 RIDER RATIFIED: the EXIT-side `PACKRAT_ARMED` assertion is
load-bearing — a cell can arm ITSELF mid-parse** (W1.md:456-463 already asserts entry AND exit;
this is its warrant). (b) M3: **K-6 AS WRITTEN (W2.md:473-474) KILLS EVERY CANDIDATE WITH A LAZY
BACK-EDGE INCLUDING THE RULED WINNER** — parse #1 pays construction by design; the saving
steady-state gloss lives only inside AC-3(d)'s predicted-failure list. **K-6 gains "in steady
state (parse #N vs parse #2)"** — one clause, or `createLazyCached` is out of the algebra §6
keeps.
⟨pt-lazy.md · LZ-8 and M3⟩

**W2.32 · pt-lazy LZ-9 / M4 (MERGED with pt-packrat M3, §1a n.5) — the flag bypass is a DESIGNED
cross-lowering invariant; dead flag machinery is the enumeration counter-example** (cl.1)
The `.parser()`-not-`.call()` bypass at `lazy.ts:22` is NOT an accident: the Rust lowering
DOCUMENTS it as a designed invariant (`lazy.rs:58-60` — "the cached inner parser never has flags
set (flags live on the outer wrapper)") that the TS side states NOWHERE. ADJUDICATED: **KEEP the
bypass, FIX `trim()`, WRITE THE INVARIANT DOWN IN TS** — routing lazy through `.call()` would
violate the cross-lowering contract, not repair a latency. **Enumeration discipline — ONE ROW, the
declared identity honoured (§1a n.5): dead flag machinery is the COUNTER-EXAMPLE CLASS** for W2's
closed-enumeration discipline (W2.md:139-142; with W2.46), booked HERE and nowhere else. LZ-9's own
disposition declares `identity: pt-packrat M3, one row`, and pt-packrat M3 is the same holding read
from the packrat side — carried whole: **`.trim()` mints two ids and burns one against the id
ceiling**, the sharpest live consequence of keeping machinery nothing writes to (its riders cash
into pt-packrat C-1 and C-8, folded at W2.45 and W3.11). W4.12 carries a CROSS-REFERENCE, not a
second booking.
⟨pt-lazy.md · LZ-9 and M4 · pt-packrat.md M3 (MERGED — one identity, §1a n.5)⟩

**W2.33 · pt-lazy LZ-1 (+K-14) — ONE cache, folded via an accessor (M1-constrained)** (cl.2)
Two unreconciled caches over one thunk: printing a lazy parser re-runs the body, mints a shadow
subtree with fresh ids memoised into `PARSER_STRINGS` under ids that can NEVER appear in packrat's
`(id,offset)` MEMO, retained for process life (the WeakMap's weakness defeated by `context.args`
holding `fn`); no `clearLazyCache()` while both siblings ship resets. **Successor spec: ONE cache,
printer folded onto the EXECUTION cache via an ACCESSOR — never `context.parser`, memo-write above
the recursion (W2.30's constraint).** Trigger bound: explicit stringification only
(`${p}` / `String(p)` / `.toString()`) — NOT `console.log`, NOT default `.debug()`. **W1 RIDER: a
harness cell must NEVER `toString()` a grammar it also identity-measures.**
⟨pt-lazy.md · LZ-1 + K-14⟩

**W2.34 · pt-lazy LZ-5 / A.6 — depth as an algebra parameter, with the consumer-act finding —
THE SOLE BOOKING OF LZ-5 / A.6, §1a n.12** (cl.2)
**ONE BOOKING (L-20 pass-3 repair)**: LZ-5 / A.6 formerly stood as a titled subject here AND in
**W2.28**, both restating the seam-suffices holding, with no merge and only a one-way pointer. It

No depth parameter, no ceiling, and NO `catch` anywhere on the parse path (exactly two
try/finally, zero catch) — the failure mode is an uncaught host RangeError escaping `parse()`,
unreachable by `recover()`'s documented promise. **G-11 owns the class (`ok:false` from a
constructed bound, never RangeError; W2.md:824-838) with the W3 rider (the breach becomes a
ParseIssue value — K-8/R-LAW-3 class; see W3.11).** A.6 ADJUDICATED: the public seam SUFFICES to
build the bounded back-edge ABOVE the library, gate-indistinguishable — no 1.1.0 ask required; a
library-side arity-2 remains the better cure and is the shape `surface-gaps.mjs:37` reds on.
The consumer-side consequence — the DEBT-3 debt owed one layer below where the band assigned it —
is pt-index m-12's, at **W2.28**, which quotes this adjudication and books it no more.
⟨pt-lazy.md · LZ-5 + superlative 6 (SOLE booking; W2.28 cross-references, §1a n.12)⟩

**W2.35 · pt-lazy A.5 REVERSAL — the incumbent is the SAME depth class (frame of record)** (cl.2;
routing annotated, §1a n.3)
RULED, reversing a reader confirmation: challenge-C A.5's "the incumbent handles every nesting
axis iteratively, no ceiling at all, none can throw a RangeError" is **FALSE** — the four entry
points it names are precisely the recursive ones (`parseValueInternal` self-recurses at
`grammar.ts:344/:354/:364/:383`; `parseStyleBody` ↔ `parseItems` mutually recurse; `collect`'s
`visit` recurses). The six `let depth` counters are the TOKENIZER'S SPLITTERS, not the tree
builder. **The honest comparison is Result-typed malformed-input signalling vs thrown ceilings IN
BOTH — the W3 totality frame, not a lazy-specific indictment.** Binds W2 Stage-3/5 comparison
discipline (no candidate argument may cite a "ceiling-free incumbent"); cross-folds to W3's G-9
frame (W3.md:504).
⟨pt-lazy.md · seat ruling 1 / K-1⟩

### 4f. pt-leaf at W2

**W2.36 · pt-leaf PT-LEAF-2 (+ -21, -22) — the sequence/undefined decision and its missing
premise** (cl.2)
THE decision the algebra turns on: **ONE sequence operator, absence EXPLICIT,
`.opt()`-under-sequence legal again, G8 retired.** Missing premise (-22): `Parser.then` KEEPS
undefined (unconditional 2-tuple) while `all()` DROPS it — two sequencing operators, two value
algebras, and leaf's fusion docblock advertises the then→all migration WITHOUT stating it is not
shape-preserving. (-21): `wrap(start,end,false)` and `.trim(p,false)` are built on `all()` and
inherit compaction whole — both first-party compositions are acceptance witnesses. Live in the
library's own GREEN suite ("e…undefined" masked by parseFloat). *Sharpens*: the sequence family
(W2.md:247).
⟨pt-leaf.md · PT-LEAF-2, -21, -22⟩

**W2.37 · pt-leaf PT-LEAF-28 — the positional law (G8 over-approximates)** (cl.2)
The drop-undefined defect is POSITIONALLY CONDITIONAL and the consumer's history proves it: a
TRAILING dropped element is accidentally safe for positional destructuring; a LEADING or MIDDLE
drop silently binds the wrong value. BOTH recorded production crashes are LEADING-position
(`index.ts:205-216` "stops is not iterable"; `math.ts:144-150` "args is not iterable" — the second
uncited by either challenge), and the surviving compensations are tail-detection at exactly the
non-trailing sites. Consequence: the blanket ban is a DEFENSIBLE BUT UNSTATED APPROXIMATION (a
structural graph-walk cannot see position) — **G8 gains that annotation and RETIRES WITH
PT-LEAF-2's cure, NOT BEFORE. W1 corpus rows for leading/middle/trailing optionals.**
⟨pt-leaf.md · PT-LEAF-28 (β-miss 2)⟩

**W2.38 · pt-leaf PT-LEAF-7 (+ PT-UTILS-2, PT-UE-22) — the trivia class fixed BY
CITATION** (cl.2)
THREE mutually inconsistent whitespace alphabets ship in one package, NONE of them CSS's
({09-0D,20} / JS `\s` incl. NBSP-BOM-LS / `<=32` incl. NUL+C0 — all three on the root barrel), and
`.trim()` silently substitutes the charCode scanner for the parser the caller passed, DISPATCHING
ON A MUTABLE PUBLIC STRING TAG. No mechanism has a complement channel: **COMP-1 UNSATISFIABLE BY
CONSTRUCTION** (W2.md:241-244). CURE-LOCK: a span-emitting `trivia` operator whose class is fixed
by a **css-syntax-3 §4.2 CITATION — NEVER HARVESTED FROM ANY IN-TREE CLASS**; all three mechanisms
retire together; **TAG-DISPATCH BANNED** (identity, not string, selects behavior). W1 rider:
non-ASCII sweep with ESCAPES, never raw glyphs. **CROSS-REFERENCE, NOT A BOOKING (L-20 pass-2
repair, §1a n.9)**: the pt-state-side statement of this same ban — **pt-state PT-STATE-7**, whose
own disposition files it as "tag-dispatch banned: identity, not a mutable string, selects behavior
(matches pt-leaf … precedent PT-LEAF-7)" — is booked ONCE at **W2.65**, which carries the killed
falsifier and the acceptance divergence **DERIVED ANALYTICALLY, NEVER EXECUTED** ("measured" struck
by the L-20 pass-3 repair, §1a n.15); this row cites it no more, exactly as W4.12 cites
W2.32.
⟨pt-leaf.md PT-LEAF-7 · pt-utils.md PT-UTILS-2 · pt-utils-entry.md PT-UE-22 (pt-state PT-STATE-7 → booked at W2.65, §1a n.9)⟩

**W2.39 · pt-leaf PT-LEAF-3 (+ PT-PARS-22, α-M-4) — terminal totality + diagnostic conservation
at EOF** (cl.2)
`regex()` rejects unconditionally at EOF WITHOUT MERGING, so every nullable pattern (incl. the
exported `whitespace`, 89 consumer refs) fails at EOF with `furthest=−1` and the rendered error
points at the BACKTRACKED offset — truncated input, the commonest authoring error, displays
offset 0. The `test()` path already handles EOF correctly, so the guard is a semantics-changing
fast exit. Rides the COMP-1 terminal-totality law; the furthest-merge ASYMMETRY becomes the
**DIAGNOSTIC-CONSERVATION LAW (merge may never destroy D — one law with W2.44)**. Public-export
reachability witness: `numberParser().parseState("")`; armed-mode sharpening: `any(regex,regex)`
at EOF yields `expected: []`. **W1 rider: EOF-inclusive corpus row.** Incumbent deletion is a
2.0.0 event (W4.14).
⟨pt-leaf.md PT-LEAF-3 · pt-parsers.md PT-PARS-22 · pt-utils.md α-M-4 rider⟩

**W2.40 · pt-leaf PT-LEAF-11 — two NAMED choice families** (cl.2)
Three spellings of "try this alternative", three post-failure offset disciplines: `any()` arity ≥2
restores, `any()` arity 1 LEAKS, `dispatch()`'s hit path NEVER CAPTURES `savedOffset` at all — a
committed choice sold by its own JSDoc as the drop-in for backtracking `any()`, with no label on
selected-arm failure. Both consumer tables survived only inside re-restoring wrappers (luck, not
contract). **CURE-LOCK: backtracking choice and committed choice become TWO NAMED OPERATOR
FAMILIES, each with a stateable EQ-5** (W2.md:247, :267); the loop-not-table correction stands —
NO TABLE-SIDE ADAPTER CAN FIX A LEAK IN THE LOOP.
⟨pt-leaf.md · PT-LEAF-11⟩

**W2.41 · pt-leaf PT-LEAF-6/-8/-10/-12/-13 — the five dispatch WRAPPER REQUIREMENTS** (cl.2)
The LUT is **RE-AUTHORED, never imported**. (i) EXPLICIT RANGE CONSTRUCTOR + throw on
reversed/ambiguous keys — any 3-char key with middle `-` is silently a range: `"+-."` (the CSS
number-start set VERBATIM) admits `,` as a value head, and the in-tree bench itself writes a range
key beside a bare `-`. (ii) Tables are ordered `Array<[key,parser]>` or Map, NEVER a plain object
— `Object.entries` integer-key hoisting makes the JSDoc's own "0-9" unoverridable by a digit key
IN EITHER INSERTION ORDER while letters obey last-wins. (iii) Non-ASCII keys REJECTED LOUDLY at
construction (out-of-bounds TypedArray writes are silent no-ops while the label still advertises
them). (iv) BOUND the intern index or widen (Int16Array, 256 B once) — 128-255 fail closed, ≥256
fail OPEN; index 255 stores as −1, byte-identical to the `fill(-1)` sentinel. (v) Heterogeneous
inference so V stays assignable under G-10 (design-forward only — both consumer receipts KILLED at
the archive bytes). (-13 re-graded MAJOR→MINOR by ruling 2.)
⟨pt-leaf.md · PT-LEAF-6, -8, -10, -12, -13⟩

**W2.42 · pt-leaf PT-LEAF-17 / -23 — the EQ-4 delivery side** (cl.2)
(17) /core ships leaf's three label CONSTRUCTIONS but withholds
`enableDiagnostics`/`mergeErrorState`, so labels are unobservable on the tier — while shipped
`dist/core.js` already pulls the packrat AND diagnostics chunks: the consumer pays for the

(W2.md:266) — leaf's PRODUCTION already complies (construction-time-constant labels, zero
error-path allocation), so the whole problem is DELIVERY-side. (23) `mergeErrorState`'s two array
assignments sit OUTSIDE the diagnostics guard — two fresh arrays per furthest-advance on the
shipping default; the armed tier's `console.error` is uninjectable. **R-LAW-3's delivery side:
diagnostics are values, allocation-free when disarmed, printed by no one.**

**W2.72 · pt-utils-entry PT-UE-6 / PT-UE-3 — the EOF cure has an ORDERING LOCK** (cl.2)
CURE-LOCK (ordering rider, source-verified): the live EOF cure is `Parser.eof()` =
`this.skip(eof())` — the `FLAG_EOF` machinery both challenges credit is UNREACHABLE DEAD CODE —
but `.eof()` DOES NOT CURE the residue defect, because skip's error arm KEEPS `state.value`, so
`.parse()` still returns residue. **THE `.parse()`/residue repair MUST LAND FIRST OR WITH IT, or
the EOF fix is INVISIBLE AT THE PUBLIC SURFACE.** EOF is a total-consumption judgment in the
algebra; incumbent `.eof()` is priced 2.0.0 (acceptance narrowing; W4.14).
⟨pt-utils-entry.md · PT-UE-6 + PT-UE-3 (α-miss ordering rider; PT-UE-3's W1 boundary witness rides W1.1 — §1a n.17)⟩

**W2.73 · pt-utils-entry PT-UE-2 — a wrong-V injector is invisible to K-1 and lethal to the
wave** (cl.2)
EQ-1 RIDER (K-1, W2.md:465-466): `escapedString`/`quotedString` do NOT unescape — `.skip()` keeps
the LEFT value so the `\uXXXX` payload is ANNIHILATED (`"\\u0041"` → `"u"`; `'"a\\u0041b"'` →
`"aub"`); only escapes whose LETTER EQUALS THEIR VALUE survive. The correct decoder ships 30 LINES
AWAY on the same subpath (`json.ts:22-24` → `"aAb"`). Two escape semantics, one 7-export surface,
both public at 1.0.0. **A wrong-`V` injector is INVISIBLE TO K-1.**
⟨pt-utils-entry.md · PT-UE-2⟩

The recursion ceiling on the shipped jsonParser is a JIT-state-dependent RANGE far below 7,761,
the throw escapes `.parse()` uncaught, AND AT THE COLD BOUNDARY THE CLASS ITSELF FLIPS:
`SyntaxError: Invalid regular expression: … Stack overflow` (V8's lazy regex compile failing under
exhausted stack, naming the library's own numeral pattern as invalid) at boundary depths,
RangeError above — a consumer's `instanceof RangeError` guard is UNSOUND and the error text points
at a grammar bug that does not exist. FOUR mutually inconsistent bad-input postures on one
surface. **W1's oracle asserts the CLASS SET and the escape path, never a depth constant** (§7
pt-utils-entry row 1; with W1.3).

**W2.78 · pt-leaf PT-LEAF-4 + PT-LEAF-9 (MERGED — one law, two witnesses) — THE OFFSET-MONOTONICITY
AND RE-ENTRANCY LAW: every operator advances or fails, and no operator reads a cursor across user
code** (cl.2 — **W2 limb recovered by the L-20 pass-4 limb sweep, §1a n.18**; belongs to §4f's
pt-leaf bloc)
PT-LEAF-9's own disposition names the merge — "same monotonicity/re-entrancy law as **PT-LEAF-4**"
(`pt-leaf.md:54`) — so the two book as ONE row. (**4**) The documented `null` arm of
`matchFunction` makes the offset delta **NEGATIVE**: a failed sticky `exec` resets `lastIndex` to
0, so a *successful* parse can rewind the cursor to absolute 0 and consume the input twice
(`["aaa","FB","aaa"]` from a four-char input); every downstream guard in the library is ONE-SIDED
(`parser.ts:538/:605`) and blind to it. (**9**) `regex()` reads the closure-singleton
`sticky.lastIndex` **AFTER invoking arbitrary user code** (`leaf.ts:322/:333/:337-340`), so a
re-entrant `matchFunction` desynchronizes value from offset — past `src.length`, after which
`eof()` spuriously succeeds. The packrat tier got epoch save/restore for exactly this class; the
leaf that hands control to user code got nothing. **FOLD: the algebra states an EXPLICIT
OFFSET-MONOTONICITY LAW over EVERY operator — `result.offset ∈ [entry, src.length]`, checked at
the operator boundary, not by one-sided caller guards — and NO operator may read a mutable cursor
across a call into consumer code; a terminal that does is not a terminal the algebra keeps
(PT-LEAF-9's `regex` RETIREs from the terminal tier, the AC-1/AC-2 host-object bet).** Both rows'
MEASURE limb is §7 pt-leaf row 2 (the monotonicity oracle, carrying K18's law that NO PROBE NUMERAL
IS PORTABLE); PT-LEAF-4's incumbent two-line delta-gate cure is 1.1.0-eligible and priced at W4.14.
⟨pt-leaf.md · PT-LEAF-4 and PT-LEAF-9 (MERGED — one law by PT-LEAF-9's own disposition, §1a n.18)⟩

**W2.79 · pt-state PT-STATE-5 + PT-STATE-10 (MERGED — one law, two faces) — `D` IS A
LAZILY-ATTACHED, WRITER-MEDIATED VALUE, NEVER TWO EAGER PUBLIC SLOTS** (cl.2 — **W2 limb recovered
by the L-20 pass-4 limb sweep, §1a n.18**; belongs to §4k's pt-state bloc)
Both rows are about the SAME two fields — `suggestions` and `secondarySpans` — read from opposite
sides. (**5**, allocation side) Two arrays per `ParserState`, allocated UNCONDITIONALLY
(`state.ts:44-45` → chunk `:301-302`) for a feature whose every writer is gated OFF by default; the
restart branch re-allocates both **unguarded** on every furthest-advance while guarding `expected`
one line above (`utils.ts:32-35`); the failure path allocates four and immediately aliases two away.
(**10**, writer side) Those same fields are **published, mutable, public array fields with ZERO
published writers on any tier** — `addSuggestion`/`addSecondarySpan` appear in no barrel (∉ the 34
root keys), so the only way a consumer can add one is a direct `.push()`, which bypasses the
`diagnosticsEnabled` gate and manufactures the exact state the module treats as impossible
(non-empty suggestions, diagnostics off) — which `parser.ts:63-64` then aliases onto the errorState
and `utils.ts:121-122` copies into a `Diagnostic`. **FOLD: in the algebra `D` is a VALUE ATTACHED
WHEN IT EXISTS and reached only through the operations that own it — never an eagerly-allocated
public slot, and never public mutable substate without a published mutator. A shape that can be
written into a state the operations declare impossible is unrepresentable, EQ-4's disqualifier
applied to the container rather than the element** (with W2.63's "no public toggle without a public
query" and W2.71's LENGTH-GUARD ruling, which is the incumbent-side cure this law makes unnecessary
for the successor). PT-STATE-5's MEASURE limb rides §7 pt-state rows 1-2 (reject-path allocation
leg; dead alias overwrite); **PT-STATE-10's `NO-WAVE-OWNER` limb — export the writers or make the
fields `readonly`, the latter a 2.0.0 event — is banked at §8 bloc 14**, giving that row a declared
two-limb residence.
⟨pt-state.md · PT-STATE-5 and PT-STATE-10 (MERGED — one law, two faces of the same two fields; PT-STATE-10's NWO limb at §8 bloc 14, §1a n.18)⟩

**W2.80 · pt-state PT-STATE-20 + pt-index M-8 (+C-M3) — R-LAW-3: RENDERING IS A FREE FUNCTION
OVER `D`, NEVER A METHOD A LOGGER CAN TRIGGER** (cl.2 — **W2 fold limb recovered by the L-20

stringifies its arguments therefore executes the renderer; attribution stays MINOR against
`state.ts` because `parser.ts` chooses to print, but **R-LAW-3 makes the print path UNPASSABLE for
any lowering**: a renderer reachable by coercion cannot be non-normative, and a wrap cannot hide a
method the language calls implicitly.
**THE pt-index CO-WITNESS, BOOKED HERE (L-20 pass-6 repair, §1a n.21).** pt-index **M-8 (+C-M3)**
is the same holding read from the index side: "arming welds an unconditional per-failed-parse

withheld from every entry; `ParserState.toString()` is the same O(n) path **unarmed**"
(`pt-index.md:63`), disposed `**W2** — R-LAW-3 (verified W2.md:281-283) owns it;
unarmed-`toString` arm as REFOLD rider`. Both of its limbs land HERE and nowhere else: the **W2
limb** is R-LAW-3 at the very lines this row folds (`W2.md:281-283`), and the **REFOLD rider** —
the unarmed `toString` path — is this row's whole subject. The two rows are **NOT declared one
identity at either source** (neither names the other), so this line is a CROSS-FOLD by n.4's rule
and not a merge: two apotheoses witnessing one law, `pt-state.md:68` from the coercion side and
`pt-index.md:63` from the arming side, and the payload cites are the same bytes (`parser.ts:67-69`
→ `debug.ts:58`). No new row is minted, because there is no second holding: M-8's remaining

**FOLD: the successor's rendering is a FREE FUNCTION taking
`D` (and its policy) as arguments; no state or result type carries `toString`/`inspect`-class

## 5. W3 — Totality and Recovery to Spec (12 sharpenings)

**W3.1 · pt-diagnostics row 18 / R2-M1 — recovered-success representation RULED BEFORE W3.b
OPENS** (cl.1)
BINDING, and it must be **RULED BEFORE THE WAVE OPENS**, not improvised mid-wave: value.js's
frozen `ParseResult<T>` forbids diagnostics on SUCCESS — the success arm is
`{ok:true; value; diagnostics: readonly []}`, an EMPTY TUPLE — so `recover()`'s entire product
(partial success carrying N diagnostics, which GROUND-B asserts GREEN) is **UNREPRESENTABLE in the
/css contract**. Both challenges analyze only the failure arm. At adoption the lowering must
discard-on-success, flip `ok:false` on a success, or reopen the frozen contract; **the choice is
recorded in the wave spec** — under E-3, that means: recorded in this addendum's execution ledger
or a dated owner ruling BEFORE W3.b dispatches. *Sharpens*: unit .b (W3.md:253-270), which
currently specifies only the failure arm's closed union.
⟨pt-diagnostics.md · roster row 18⟩

**W3.2 · pt-diagnostics row 7 / L·B-3+C·D-18 — mark/rewind per R-LAW-1 (BLOCKER, kill REVERSED)**
(cl.2)
`recover()`'s rollback is an unqualified LIFO `pop()`, not a mark/rewind: under the canonical
two-level composition (outer recover whose sync itself recovers — EXACTLY a resilient CSS
stylesheet's block/declaration shape) a failed outer sync DESTROYS the inner's legitimate record
and RETAINS the outer record it promised to remove, silently, length restored. LIVE WITNESS P3
(neither reader ran it): journal = 1 where `parser.ts:673` promises 0, survivor = D_outer.
**W3.b implements mark/rewind per R-LAW-1 (record journal length before sync, truncate on
failure), per W2.md:275-278.** (W3.b's current text, W3.md:253-270, carries no mark/rewind clause
— this row adds it.)
⟨pt-diagnostics.md · roster row 7 + Seat ruling 1⟩

**W3.3 · pt-diagnostics rows 8+9 — the EQ-5 rollback-exactness rider** (cl.1)
`collectDiagnostic` ends in `resetErrorState` on the LIVE parse state (`furthest = −1`) and
NEITHER recover exit path restores it; the failed-sync path — whose whole purpose is to let the
error propagate untouched — propagates a state whose furthest/expected/suggestions/secondarySpans
were already destroyed (`popLastDiagnostic` restores the BUFFER, nothing restores the STATE; the
`parser.ts:673` comment is true of the journal and false of the state). Live witness P4: furthest
2 → 0, unarmed. **BINDING on W3.b's mark/rewind: snapshot {furthest, expected, suggestions,
secondarySpans} around `sync`, not just journal length.**
⟨pt-diagnostics.md · roster rows 8 and 9 (MERGED — 9 folds via 8)⟩

**W3.4 · pt-parser PT-PARSER-3 — recovery as an append-only per-parse VALUE (BLOCKER, LIVE)**
(cl.2)
SEVERITY RAISED because THE COMBINATOR IS LIVE: the band's only real parse-that consumer wires
`.recover()` and asserts `getCollectedDiagnostics()` UNARMED, with two PASSING vitest assertions —
L-M1's "write-only" premise is DEAD and L-B3's own "unreachable in practice" falsifier is refuted.
`recover()` pushes into a module-global `Diagnostic[]` with NO IDENTITY on the record and pops
POSITIONALLY on sync failure; the buffer is OUTSIDE the packrat epoch (PackratEpoch has five
members, not six); the pop path leaves the outer parse's furthest DESTROYED. **W3.b's mark/rewind
snapshots the FOUR state fields, not just journal length** (one discipline with W3.2/W3.3).
⟨pt-parser.md · PT-PARSER-3 + seat ruling 2 / SEAT-K2⟩

**W3.5 · pt-diagnostics row 19 / R2-M2 — R-LAW-5 declared + the non-final-arm corpus row** (cl.2)
R-LAW-5 — the ONE recovery law that names `recover` (W2.md:287-290) — appears ZERO times in either
challenge doc, and live parse-that violates its declaration requirement: a recovered branch
returns `state.ok(sentinel)` and `any()` returns on the first non-error arm, so a recovering
NON-FINAL alternative silently STARVES every later arm. Nothing in README declares it; zero tests
pin it. Lands on the band: both candidates are any/dispatch-shaped. **W3.b declares the
alt/recover rule (forbid non-final-arm recovery or declare the alternative); W1 RIDER: the
malformed corpus gains a non-final-arm recovery construction so the G-4 probe can bite** (§7
pt-diagnostics row 4).
⟨pt-diagnostics.md · roster row 19 + MEASURE-AT-OPEN 4⟩

**W3.6 · pt-diagnostics row 25 (+26) / C·D-12 — Diagnostic ↛ ParseIssue: NO total map** (cl.1)
Diagnostic is structurally non-conformant with frozen ParseIssue on all five EQ-4 fields: no
`code`, two positions with no invariant, no `end` (unrecoverable — `found` clamps at 20),
armed-only `expected`, `found` where `actual: string|null` is wanted. **NO total
information-preserving map exists. W3.b's codes/diagnostics unit maps furthestOffset → start and
derives `end` from its OWN span discipline; NOTHING maps from Diagnostic.** Rider:
`Diagnostic.offset` is write-only — record it as un-interpreted, never map it (with W2.12).
**THE SOLE BOOKING OF ROW 26 (L-20 pass-4 repair, §1a n.17)**: row 26 is source-declared "Rider on
row 25's fold", and this is row 25's fold; W2.12 formerly also carried it in provenance and now

*Sharpens*: unit .b's `{lower,diagnostics,codes}.ts` (W3.md:268).

**W3.7 · pt-diagnostics row 27 / C·D-15 — recovery-skipped bytes enter C as spans** (cl.1)
`found` is lossy four ways on one line (fixed UTF-16 cut with a lone-surrogate hazard,
single-character escape list letting `\r`/`\t`/`\x1b` into ANSI context, a fresh RegExp per
record, and NO extent field anywhere) — the shape's only byte-level record cannot carry

nothing ports it.**

**W3.8 · pt-parser PT-PARSER-16 — the ParseIssue adoption arithmetic, PRICED BEFORE THE WAVE**
(cl.1)
BINDING as EXPLICIT PRICED OBLIGATIONS, not discoveries-at-open: parse-that's default-path failure
state can produce AT MOST ONE of value.js's five frozen ParseIssue fields. `start` derivable
(`parser.ts:60`); **`end` has NO PRODUCER ANYWHERE** (ParserState carries offsets only;
SecondarySpan is a point; the 15 `*Span` builders were excised at 1.0.0 — a KNOWN delegation per
CHANGELOG r6 #8, but NO corpus row priced the frozen field); `expected` is armed-only;
`actual`/`found` exists only via `collectDiagnostic` — an O-8 breach to obtain. **W2 §3b and W3's
typed-recovery unit each carry the three missing producers.**
⟨pt-parser.md · PT-PARSER-16 (β-miss 2)⟩

**W3.9 · pt-debug B-2 + MISS-1 + i-4 — one line/column law, one implementation (BLOCKER)** (cl.2)
`state.ts:128`'s `lastIndexOf("\n", offset−1)` clamps at offset 0 per ECMA-262, so a leading
newline is FOUND: `"\nabc"` → `{line:2, column:−1}` where truth is `{1,0}`. MISS-1 is the decisive
fact: `formatDiagnostic`'s HEADER prints the CORRECT `1:0` from `collectDiagnostic`'s slice-first
math while the caret block underneath recomputes through the broken helper — **one rendered
diagnostic contradicts itself**. **CURE DIRECTION IS RULED: install the slice-first algorithm INTO
`state.ts`, never the reverse** (the reverse cure injects a negative column into every offset-0
Diagnostic). Three implementations, two dead, disagreeing. *Sharpens*: W3.b + G-3/G-8
(W3.md:398, :487).
⟨pt-debug.md B-2 / MISS-1 / i-4 · pt-diagnostics.md rows 20/30 + K1 · pt-core.md row 11 (PRIMARY; its in-library cure stays EXCLUDED, §8)⟩

**W3.10 · pt-packrat C-3 — disarm-and-refuse-mid-grow** (cl.2)
Public `resetPackrat()` can CRASH a live grow (three non-null asserts on a clearable Map with user
code on the stack via `.map`/`.chain`/`.mapState`/`recover` under `evalParser`) and is
non-load-bearing everywhere in-tree, while the module header still INSTRUCTS the call. **W3's

clearing.**

**Hoisted law, stated once** (each module row below inherits it whole): **one fresh process per
cell · one module format per cell (W1.12) · nothing armed in any timing cell · every
arming/probing PID is a POISON CELL — disposable, never reused in a timing cell · NO PROBE NUMERAL
IS PORTABLE (the pt-leaf K18 law) · no number below is a bar (the bar is OWNER-GATED,
W1.md:515-527) · absolute figures RETIRED by the apotheoses STAY RETIRED here — ratios between
interleaved cells only (W1.md:441-448) · non-ASCII rows use ESCAPES, never raw glyphs · every
census states its counting unit beside its pattern · chunk names re-derived from the dist entry's
import specifier at the moment of use (W1.15) · consumer-root HEAD receipts re-resolved at use
(W1.20).**

  25.3%) are an esbuild re-bundle, not statically derivable; re-run before any byte figure is
  banked forward. (2) The adoption acceptance probe (build value.js ./css against a parse-that
  substrate; probe with W4.9's corrected spelling → expect 0) has NEVER RUN. (3) The
  `enabled === true` arm has NEVER been observed in any session (every seat's stderr was piped) —
  one cell under a real PTY witnesses both redirection directions (`> file` polluted; `2> file`
  de-coloured). (4) `parsethat-surface-gaps.mjs` RED-7 figure — the prober ARMS diagnostics and

  by any core seat; re-measure BEFORE the figure is banked into any gate or cure justification.
  (2) PT-04 depth ceiling — a stack-budget property, not a library constant: 7,761 (O-15) and
  ≥9,000 (L) are machine-facts of DIFFERENT CONSTRUCTIONS; G-9 asserts a typed bound exists, NEVER

  per-construction/per-advance arrays, HOLEY arms) — all unmeasured; harness rows only if a cure

## 8. EXCLUDED register (cl.4) — collated, deliberately NOT folded

Each row is banked, not lost: the registers below name where the debt lives and why no X·P wave
owns it. The O-15 channel is **owner-held**; parse-that's tree, manifest, gates, and docs are
**outside every X·P wave scope** (W2.md:193-197; W4.md:132-140); the W4.14 semver law prices
every ride.

1. **pt-packrat-entry K5 — RETIRE-AS-REDUNDANT, never RETIRE-AS-CURE**: an OWNER DECISION on the
   O-15 channel, priced at one major by C-3's own precedent, retirement condition MEASURED-MET;
   every cited clause binds the CANDIDATE, not a third party's export map. Not folded into any
   wave.
2. **pt-packrat-entry C-1≡E-M1, E-M2, E-M3, C-6≡E-M5, M-1, M-5 + the MINOR/INFO tail** *(that last
   phrase is a SEVERITY-CLASS quantifier and is **SUPERSEDED** by the id enumeration below — pass 6,
   2026-08-08, §1a n.21; read the enumeration, never the quantifier)*: all four
   surviving MAJORs are parse-that-side and NO-WAVE-OWNER (nominal tier boundary at 91.1% of the
   library by evaluated bytes; 2-of-3 names one function; a reset that neither resets nor disarms;
   the self-contradicting existsSync-only gate that CONCEALED M-1; the untypable `require`
   condition). No X·P wave owns parse-that's manifest, gates, or docs.
   **THE "MINOR/INFO TAIL" EXPANDED TO IDS, ROW BY ROW AT THE BYTES — NINE MEMBERS TRIMMED
   (L-20 pass-6 repair, 2026-08-08, §1a n.21).** This bloc was the one register entry naming its
   rows by SEVERITY CLASS instead of by id. A class-quantifier is unfalsifiable and unsweepable —
   n.17's literal §8 expansion and n.20's range sweep both slid past it — so it is replaced here by
   the enumeration, built by reading pt-packrat-entry's adjudicated roster and its MISSED roster at
   the bytes and reading each row's terminal disposition for a NO-WAVE-OWNER (`NWO`) limb, which is
   the only limb this bloc is entitled to bank.
   **THE TAIL IS FIFTEEN ROWS, EVERY ONE `NWO` AT SOURCE**: **C-3** (identity arm) · **E-B1**
   (fact arm) · **E-M4** · **E-M6** · **E-m1≡C-5** · **E-m2** · **C-8** · **C-9** · **C-7** ·
   **E-m4** · **E-m5** · **E-m6** · **C-4** · **E-m3** · **M-4**. Three of the fifteen are ALSO
